(5.26)

Remark 5.8
.

The algebras
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta})^{\rm tw}
and
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{\rm tw}
may be considered as flat deformations of the twisted current algebras
{\mathcal{U}}(\mathfrak{g}[u]^{\theta})
and
{\mathcal{U}}(\mathfrak{g}[u^{2}])
, respectively. It is clear that
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta})^{\rm tw}/\hbar{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta})^{\rm tw}

\cong{\mathcal{U}}(\mathfrak{g}[u]^{\theta})
and
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{\rm tw}/\hbar{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{\rm tw}\cong{\mathcal{U}}(\mathfrak{g}[u^{2}])
. The flatness of the deformations then follows from the Poincaré–Birkhoff–Witt (PBW) theorem which is due the fact that both twisted Yangians can be embedded to the Yangian
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
; the PBW theorem for
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
was demonstrated in
[
31
]
.

The proof of Theorems
5.5
and
5.6
follows using the same arguments outlined in
[
13
, Section 21.1]
. The uniqueness of co-actions (
5.15
) and (
5.23
), (
5.24
) is demonstrated in Sections
7.3
and
7.4
; in particular, the co-action
\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}
is determined uniquely (up to an isomorphism discussed in the paragraph below) by the coideal compatibility identities (
5.1
), (
5.2
) and the property (4) of Definition
5.3
. The map (
5.16
) is the MacKay twisted Yangian formula presented in
[
16
]
. The challenging task is to obtain the horrific relations (
5.11
), (
5.12
) and (
5.18
), which are quantizations of (
3.7
), (
3.8
) and (
3.10
), respectively. A proof of the first two relations is given in Section
7.5
. Proving (
5.18
) is substantially more difficult. We have given an outline of a proof in Section
7.6
.

Recall that the Yangian
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
has a one-parameter group of automorphisms
\varkappa_{c}
, with
c\in\mathbb{C}
, given by
\varkappa_{c}(x_{a})=x_{a}
and
\varkappa_{c}({\mathcal{J}}(x_{a}))={\mathcal{J}}(x_{a})+\hbar cx_{a}
, which is compatible with both algebra and Hopf algebra structure. An analogue of this automorphism for the twisted Yangians is a one-parameter family of co-actions
(\varkappa_{c}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}
, which for
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta})^{tw}
is equivalent to one-parameter family of embeddings
\varkappa_{c}\circ\varphi
; the later does not apply to
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{tw}
due to the
\hbar
-order term in (
5.24
).

Remark 5.9
.

We strongly believe that expressions in (
5.19
)–(
5.21
) could be further reduced to a more elegant and compact form. We have succeeded to find such a form when
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
:

\displaystyle\Psi_{abc}^{ijk}=\tfrac{1}{3}\mathcal{A}_{(abc)}^{ijk}+\alpha_{(ab}^{\hskip 8.19447ptd}\alpha_{c)l}^{\;\;\,\;k}\phi_{d}^{\;lij}-\alpha_{dl}^{\;\;\,k}\alpha_{(ab}^{\hskip 8.19447ptd}\phi_{c)}^{\;lij},

\displaystyle\Phi_{abc}^{ijk}=-\tfrac{1}{6}\mathcal{A}_{abc}^{ijk},\qquad\overline{\Phi}_{abc}^{\,ijkln}=\tfrac{1}{36}\alpha_{(a}^{\;\;\,ir}\alpha_{b}^{\;js}\mathcal{A}_{c)rs}^{\,klm}.

6
Coideal subalgebras of the Yangian
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3})}

In this section we present three examples of twisted Yangians
{\mathcal{Y}}\big(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta}\big)^{\rm tw}
when
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
and
\mathfrak{g}^{\theta}=\mathfrak{so}_{3}
or
\mathfrak{g}^{\theta}=\mathfrak{gl}_{2}
(both
\theta\neq\operatorname{id}
cases), or
\mathfrak{g}^{\theta}=\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
(
\theta=\operatorname{id}
case). For ease of notation we will denote generators of the former two algebras by symbols
\mathsf{h}
,
\mathsf{e}
,
\mathsf{f}
,
\mathsf{k}
and
\mathsf{H}
,
\mathsf{E}
,
\mathsf{F}
. We start by recalling the Chevalley–Serre presentation of the
\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
Lie algebra and its Yangian.

6.1
The
\boldsymbol{\mathfrak{sl}_{3}}
Lie algebra and the Yangian
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3})}

Lie algebra
\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
in the Chevalley–Serre presentation is generated by elements
e_{i}
,
f_{i}
,
h_{i}
with
i=1,2
subject to the defining relations

\displaystyle[e_{i},f_{j}]=\delta_{ij}h_{i},\qquad[h_{i},e_{j}]=a_{ij}e_{j},\qquad[h_{i},f_{j}]=-a_{ij}f_{j},

\displaystyle[e_{i},[e_{i},e_{i\pm 1}]]=0,\qquad[f_{i},[f_{i},f_{i\pm 1}]]=0,

(6.1)

where
a_{ii}=2
and
a_{12}=-1=a_{21}
are the matrix elements of the Cartan matrix of
\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
. The last two relations are called the Serre relations.

Vector space basis of
\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
contains eight elements. In addition to the elements given above there are two more root vectors that correspond to the non-simple roots of
\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
, namely
e_{3}=[e_{1},e_{2}]
and
f_{3}=[f_{2},f_{1}]
. The defining relations for the linear basis are obtained by dropping the Serre relations in (
6.1
) and adding

\displaystyle[e_{1},e_{2}]=e_{3},\qquad[e_{1},e_{3}]=[e_{2},e_{3}]=0,\qquad[f_{1},f_{2}]=-f_{3},\qquad[f_{1},f_{3}]=[f_{2},f_{3}]=0,

\displaystyle[e_{1},f_{3}]=-f_{2},\qquad[e_{2},f_{3}]=f_{1},\qquad[f_{1},e_{3}]=e_{2},\quad[f_{2},e_{3}]=-e_{1},

\displaystyle[h_{i},f_{3}]=-f_{3},\qquad[h_{i},e_{3}]=e_{3},\qquad[e_{3},f_{3}]=h_{1}+h_{2}.

(6.2)

The quadratic Casimir operator is
C_{\mathfrak{g}}=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}(e_{i}f_{i}+f_{i}e_{i})+\frac{2}{3}\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq 2}h_{i}h_{j}
and
\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}=6
.

Example 6.1
.

The Yangian
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3})
is the unital associative algebra with sixteen generators
e_{i}
,
f_{i}
,
h_{j}
,
{\mathcal{J}}(e_{i})
,
{\mathcal{J}}(f_{i})
,
{\mathcal{J}}(h_{j})
with
i=1,2,3
and
j=1,2
. The defining relations (
5.5
), (
5.6
) are given by (
6.1
), (
6.2
) and

\displaystyle[{\mathcal{J}}(h_{1}),{\mathcal{J}}(h_{2})]=\tfrac{3}{4}\hbar^{2}(\{e_{3},f_{1},f_{2}\}-\{e_{1},e_{2},f_{3}\}).

(6.3)

The remaining relations defined by (
5.6
) are obtained by the adjoint action of degree-
0
generators on (
6.3
). (The same applies to all horrific relations in the examples given below.) The Hopf algebra structure on
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3})
is given by (
5.8
), (
5.9
) with
\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}=\sum_{1\leq i\leq 3}(e_{i}\otimes f_{i}+f_{i}\otimes e_{i})+\frac{1}{3}\sum_{1\leq i\leq j\leq 2}(h_{i}\otimes h_{j}+h_{j}\otimes h_{i})
.

6.2
Twisted Yangian
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3},\mathfrak{so}_{3})^{\rm tw}}

Let involution
\theta
be defined by

\displaystyle\theta\colon\ e_{1}\mapsto-e_{2},\qquad f_{1}\mapsto-f_{2},\qquad h_{1}\mapsto h_{2}.

The action of
\theta
on the remaining elements of
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
is deduced from the constraint
\theta^{2}=\operatorname{id}
. The symmetric space basis is given by
\mathfrak{g}^{\theta}(=\mathfrak{h})=\{\mathsf{h}=h_{1}+h_{2},\mathsf{e}=e_{1}-e_{2},\mathsf{f}=f_{1}-f_{2}\}
and
\mathfrak{m}=\{h_{1}-h_{2},e_{1}+e_{2},f_{1}+f_{2},e_{3},f_{3}\}
. The positive eigenspace of
\theta
forms the orthogonal subalgebra
\mathfrak{so}_{3}\subset\mathfrak{sl}_{3}
. We denote the generators of this subalgebra by
\mathsf{h}
,
\mathsf{e}
,
\mathsf{f}
. They generate the degree-0 subalgebra of the twisted Yangian
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{sl}_{3},\mathfrak{so}_{3})^{\rm tw}
. We denote the degree-1 generators by
\mathsf{H}
,
\mathsf{E}
,
\mathsf{F}
,
\mathsf{E}_{2}
,
\mathsf{F}_{2}
.

7
Proofs

In the remaining part of the paper we provide proofs omitted in the previous sections.

7.1
A proof of Proposition
3.1

We follow the arguments outlined in
[
13
, Section 12.1]
and fill in the gaps. Recall that given a simple Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}
the half-loop Lie algebra
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
is generated by the elements
x^{(k)}_{a}
with
k\geq 0
and
a=1,\ldots,\dim(\mathfrak{g})
, and satisfying (
3.1
). Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{L}}}^{+}
denote the algebra generated by the elements
x_{a}
and
J(x_{a})
satisfying (
3.2
)–(
3.4
). The map
\varphi\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{+}\to{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
given by
x_{a}\mapsto x_{a}^{(0)}
,
J(x_{a})\mapsto x_{a}^{(1)}
is an algebra homomorphism. It is a direct computation to check that the image of (
3.2
)–(
3.4
) holds in
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
.

To prove that
\varphi
is surjective we need to show that elements
x^{(0)}_{a}
and
x^{(1)}_{a}
generate the whole
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
. Indeed, since
\mathfrak{g}
is simple, we have
[\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}]=\mathfrak{g}
, or equivalently
\big[{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)},{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}\big]={\mathcal{L}}^{(2)}
, where
{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}=\operatorname{span}\big\{x_{a}^{(k)}\big\}
. By the same arguments we must have, for all
k>0
, that
\big[{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)},{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}\big]={\mathcal{L}}^{(k+1)}
. This shows that
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
is generated by
{\mathcal{L}}^{(0)}
and
{\mathcal{L}}^{(1)}
. Hence it only remains to show that
\varphi
is injective.

We know that
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}
, with
\dim\big({\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}\big)=\dim(\mathfrak{g})
, as a vector space. We need to show that an analogous statement holds for
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
. Denote
J^{(0)}(x_{a})=x_{a}
,
J^{(1)}(x_{a})=J(x_{a})
and define recursively

\displaystyle J^{(k)}(x_{a})=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{a}^{bc}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{c}),J^{(k-1)}(x_{b})\big]\qquad\text{for all}\quad k\geq 1.

(7.1)

We need to show that

\displaystyle\big[J^{(\ell)}(x_{a}),J^{(k-\ell)}(x_{b})\big]=\alpha_{ab}^{\;\;\,c}J^{(k)}(x_{c})\qquad\text{for all}\quad\ell\leq k,

(7.2)

so that
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{+}=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}
with
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}=\operatorname{span}\{J^{(k)}(x_{a})\}
and
\dim(\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)})=\dim(\mathfrak{g})
. We first demonstrate that

\displaystyle\big[J^{(k-\ell)}(x_{a}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{b})\big]=\big[J^{(k-\ell^{\prime})}(x_{a}),J^{(\ell^{\prime})}(x_{b})\big]\qquad\text{for all}\quad\ell<\ell^{\prime}\leq k.

(7.3)

We proceed by induction on
k
, the result being clear when
k=0,1
, by (
3.2
). For
k=2
there are two inequivalent identities that need to be shown: with
\ell=0
,
\ell^{\prime}=2
and with
\ell=0
,
\ell^{\prime}=1
. The first identity follows by (
7.1
) and co-Jacobi identity:

\displaystyle\big[J^{(2)}(x_{a}),J^{(0)}(x_{b})\big]=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{a}^{\;dc}\big[\big[J^{(1)}(x_{c}),J^{(1)}(x_{d})\big],J^{(0)}(x_{b})\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\big[J^{(2)}(x_a),J^{(0)}(x_b)\big]}{}=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{a}^{\;dc}\big(\alpha_{cb}^{\;\;\,e}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{e}),J^{(1)}(x_{d})\big]+\alpha_{db}^{\;\;\,e}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{c}),J^{(1)}(x_{e})\big]\big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{\big[J^{(2)}(x_a),J^{(0)}(x_b)\big]}{}=\alpha_{ab}^{\;\;\,c}J^{(2)}(x_{c}).

In a similar way we obtain that
\big[J^{(0)}(x_{a}),J^{(2)}(x_{b})\big]=\alpha_{ab}^{\;\;\,c}J^{(2)}(x_{c})
thus yielding the required identity. To obtain the second identity we need to contract (
3.3
) with
\alpha_{d}^{\;cb}
giving

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{a}),J^{(1)}(x_{d})\big]+\alpha_{d}^{\;cb}\alpha_{ca}^{\;\;\,e}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{b}),J^{(1)}(x_{e})\big]+\alpha_{d}^{\;cb}\alpha_{ab}^{\;\;\,e}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{c}),J^{(1)}(x_{e})\big]=0.

Now rename the indices
b\to c
,
c\to e
,
e\to b
in the third term and use co-Jacobi identity together with (
7.1
). This gives
\big[J^{(1)}(x_{a}),J^{(1)}(x_{d})\big]=\alpha_{ad}^{\;\;\,c}J^{(2)}(x_{c})=\big[J^{(0)}(x_{a}),J^{(2)}(x_{d})\big]
. Next, assuming inductively (
7.3
), we have

\displaystyle\big[J^{(s)}(x_{c}),\big[J^{(r-\ell)}(x_{a}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{a})\big]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\alpha_{ca}^{\;\;\,b}\big(\big[J^{(r-\ell+s)}(x_{b}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{a})\big]+\big[J^{(r-\ell)}(x_{a}),J^{(\ell+s)}(x_{b})\big]\big)=0

for all
\ell\leq r\leq k
,
r-\ell+s\leq k
and
\ell+s\leq k
giving

\displaystyle\big[J^{(r-\ell+s)}(x_{b}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{a})\big]=\big[J^{(\ell+s)}(x_{b}),J^{(r-\ell)}(x_{a})\big].

Taking all the allowed
\ell
,
r
,
s
such that
r+s\leq k+1
we obtain all the necessary identities thus completing the induction.

We are now ready to prove (
7.2
). We use induction on
k
. The cases with
k\leq 2
are discussed above. Thus, by induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle\big[J^{(1)}(x_{d}),\big[J^{(k-\ell)}(x_{a}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{b})\big]\big]=\alpha_{ab}^{\;\;\,c}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{d}),J^{(k)}(x_{c})\big].

Now contract the lhs with
\alpha_{e}^{\;ad}
, use (
7.1
), (
7.3
) and co-Jacobi identity:

\displaystyle\alpha_{e}^{\;ad}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{d}),\big[J^{(k-\ell)}(x_{a}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{b})\big]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big[J^{(k-\ell+1)}(x_{e}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{b})\big]+\alpha_{e}^{\;ad}\alpha_{db}^{\;\;\,f}\big[J^{(k-\ell)}(x_{a}),J^{(\ell+1)}(x_{f})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big[J^{(k-\ell+1)}(x_{e}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{b})\big]-\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\alpha_{eb}^{\;\;\,d}J^{(k+1)}(x_{d}).

By doing the same for the rhs we get

\displaystyle\alpha_{e}^{\;ad}\alpha_{ab}^{\;\;\,c}\big[J^{(1)}(x_{d}),J^{(k)}(x_{c})\big]=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\alpha_{eb}^{\;\;\,a}J^{(k+1)}(x_{a})

yielding
\big[J^{(k-\ell+1)}(x_{e}),J^{(\ell)}(x_{b})\big]=\alpha_{eb}^{\;\;\,d}J^{(k+1)}(x_{d})
, which completes the induction. Therefore
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{+}=\bigoplus\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{(k)}
. By setting
\varphi\colon J^{(k)}(x_{a})\mapsto x^{(k)}_{a}
we obtain a bijection of vector spaces, hence
\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}^{+}\cong{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
.

7.2
A proof of Proposition
3.2

The idea behind the proof is very similar to that of the prove above. Recall that
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
is the subalgebra of
{\mathcal{L}}^{+}
generated by elements
X^{(2k)}_{\alpha}
and
Y^{(2k+1)}_{p}
with
k\geq 0
and satisfying (
3.5
). Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{H}}}^{+}
denote the algebra generated by the elements
X_{\alpha}
and
B(Y_{p})
satisfying (
3.6
)–(
3.8
). The map
\psi\colon\widetilde{{\mathcal{H}}}^{+}\to{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
given by
X_{\alpha}\mapsto X_{\alpha}^{(0)}
,
B(Y_{p})\mapsto Y_{p}^{(1)}
is an algebra homomorphism. It is easy to see that the image of (
3.6
) holds in
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
. To see that the same is true for (
3.7
) and (
3.8
) we additionally need to use (
2.9
) and (
2.10
).

To prove that
\psi
is surjective we need to show that elements
X^{(0)}_{\alpha}
and
Y^{(1)}_{p}
, where
X_{\alpha}
runs over a basis for
\mathfrak{h}
and
Y_{p}
runs over a basis for
\mathfrak{m}
, generate the whole
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
. Indeed, since
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{m}\oplus\mathfrak{h}
is simple, we must have
[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}]=\mathfrak{h}
and
[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{h}]=\mathfrak{m}
, or equivalently
\big[{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)},{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}\big]={\mathcal{H}}^{(2)}
and
\big[{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)},{\mathcal{H}}^{(2)}\big]={\mathcal{H}}^{(3)}
, where
{\mathcal{H}}^{(2k)}=\operatorname{span}\big\{X_{\alpha}^{(2k)}\big\}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{(2k+1)}=\operatorname{span}\big\{Y_{p}^{(2k+1)}\big\}
. By the same arguments we must have, for all
k>0
, that
\big[{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)},{\mathcal{H}}^{(k)}\big]={\mathcal{H}}^{(k+1)}
. This shows that
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
is generated by
{\mathcal{H}}^{(0)}
and
{\mathcal{H}}^{(1)}
. It remains to show that
\psi
is injective.

We know that
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}{\mathcal{H}}^{(k)}
, with
\dim\big({\mathcal{H}}^{(2k)}\big)=\dim(\mathfrak{h})
and
\dim\big({\mathcal{H}}^{(2k+1)}\big)=\dim(\mathfrak{m})
, as a vector space. We need to show that an analogous statement holds for
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
. Let
B^{(0)}(X_{\alpha})=X_{\alpha}
,
B^{(1)}(Y_{p})=B(Y_{p})
and define recursively, for
k\geq 0
,

\displaystyle B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha})=(\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\alpha)})^{-1}w_{\alpha}^{\;qp}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{q})\big],

(7.4)

\displaystyle B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{p})=2\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}g_{p}^{\;\alpha q}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{q}),B^{(2k)}(X_{\alpha})\big].

(7.5)

We need to show that, for all
\ell\leq k
,

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell)}(X_{\beta})\big]={f}^{\;\;\,\gamma}_{\alpha\beta}B^{(2k)}(X_{\gamma}),

(7.6)

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big]=g^{\;\;\,q}_{\alpha p}B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{q}),

(7.7)

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w^{\;\;\,\alpha}_{pq}B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha}),

(7.8)

so that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{+}=\bigoplus_{k\geq 0}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(k)}
, with
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(2k)}=\operatorname{span}\big\{B^{(2k)}(X_{\alpha})\big\}
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(2k+1)}=\operatorname{span}\big\{B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{p})\big\}
, as a vector space.

We start by showing (
7.6
)–(
7.8
) for small
k
that follow from (
3.6
)–(
3.8
). First, notice that (
7.4
) implies that (
3.7
) is equivalent to
\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(1)}(Y_{q})\big]=\sum_{\alpha}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha})
. Also, notice that, by (
2.8
), (
2.9
) and (
7.4
),

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(1)}(Y_{r})\big]=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}(\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\alpha)})^{-1}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}w_{\alpha}^{\;st}\big[\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{t}),B^{(1)}(Y_{s})],B^{(1)}(Y_{r})\big]\\
&\hphantom{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(1)}(Y_{r})\big]}{}=\big[\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(1)}(Y_{q})\big],B^{(1)}(Y_{r})\big].\end{split}

Then, by (
2.8
), (
2.10
) and (
7.4
), (
7.5
),

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,s}B^{(3)}(Y_{s})=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha,\beta}2(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}})^{-1}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,s}g_{s}^{\;u\beta}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\beta}),B^{(1)}(Y_{u})\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha} g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,s} B^{(3)}(Y_{s})}{}=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha,\beta}2(\kappa_{\mathfrak{m}})^{tu}(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\beta)})^{-1}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,\,s}w_{st}^{\;\;\,\beta}w_{\beta}^{\;vx}\big[\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{x}),B^{(1)}(Y_{v})\big],B^{(1)}(Y_{u})\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha} g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,s} B^{(3)}(Y_{s})}{}=2\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}(\kappa_{\mathfrak{m}})^{tu}w_{pq}^{\;\;\,\alpha}g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,\,s}\big[\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{s}),B^{(1)}(Y_{t})\big],B^{(1)}(Y_{u})\big].

By combining the expressions above with (
3.8
) we obtain
\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(1)}(Y_{r})\big]=g_{\alpha r}^{\;\;\,\,s}B^{(3)}(Y_{s})
. Consequently, we have that

\displaystyle w_{\beta}^{\;rp}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(1)}(Y_{r})\big]\big]=w_{\beta}^{\;rp}g_{\alpha r}^{\;\;\,\,s}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(3)}(Y_{s})\big]

giving

\displaystyle w_{\beta}^{\;rp}g_{p\alpha}^{\;\;\,q}\big[B^{(3)}(Y_{q}),B^{(1)}(Y_{r})\big]+\textstyle\sum\limits_{\gamma}w_{\beta}^{\;rp}w_{pr}^{\;\;\,\gamma}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2)}(X_{\gamma})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=w_{\beta}^{\;pr}g_{\alpha p}^{\;\;\,\,q}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{r}),B^{(3)}(Y_{q})\big].

Using (
2.6
), (
2.9
), (
7.4
), (
7.5
) and antisymmetry in
\alpha
and
\beta
we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\beta)}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2)}(X_{\beta})\big]=\big(w_{\beta}^{\;pr}g_{\alpha p}^{\;\;\,\,q}-w_{\alpha}^{\;pr}g_{\beta p}^{\;\;\,q}\big)\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{r}),B^{(3)}(Y_{q})\big]=\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\beta)}f_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\,\gamma}B^{(4)}(X_{\gamma}),

which gives
\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2)}(X_{\beta})\big]=f_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\,\gamma}B^{(4)}(X_{\gamma})
. Then, a direct computation using (
7.4
), (
7.5
) yields
\big[B^{(0)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2)}(X_{\alpha})\big]=f_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\,\gamma}B^{(2)}(X_{\gamma})
and
\big[B^{(0)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(3)}(Y_{r})\big]=g_{\alpha r}^{\;\;\,\,s}B^{(3)}(Y_{s})
and finally
\big[B^{(0)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(4)}(X_{\beta})\big]=f_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\,\gamma}B^{(4)}(X_{\gamma})
.

Next we show the following identities that will be necessary in proving (
7.7
) and (
7.8
):

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big]=\big[B^{(2k-2\ell^{\prime})}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell^{\prime}+1)}(Y_{p})\big],

(7.9)

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]=\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big].

(7.10)

We proceed by induction on
k
, the result being clear when
k=0
and when
k=1
for (
7.9
). Assuming induction we have

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2s)}(X_{\alpha}),\big[B^{(2r-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=g_{\alpha p}^{\;\;\,\;q}\big(\big[B^{(2r-2\ell+2s+1)}(Y_{q}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big]+\big[B^{(2r-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+2s+1)}(Y_{q})\big]\big)=0

for all
\ell\leq r\leq k
,
r-\ell+s\leq k
and
\ell+s\leq k
, therefore we must have

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2r+2s-2\ell+1)}(Y_{q}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big]=\big[B^{(2s+2\ell+1)}(Y_{q}),B^{(2r-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big].

Taking all the allowed
\ell
,
r
,
s
such that
r+s\leq k+1
we obtain all the necessary identities thus completing the induction. Then, by (
7.4
), it follows that, for all
\ell\leq k
,

\displaystyle B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha})=(\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\alpha)})^{-1}w_{\alpha}^{\;qp}\big[B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big].

(7.11)

Relation (
7.9
) is proved similarly, using

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2s+1)}(Y_{q}),\big[B^{(2r-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w_{qp}^{\;\;\,\alpha}\big(\big[B^{(2r-2\ell+2s+2)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p})\big]+\big[B^{(2r-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+2s+2)}(X_{\alpha})\big]\big)=0

and implies that, for all
\ell\leq k
,

\displaystyle B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{p})=2\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}g_{p}^{\;\alpha q}\big[B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q}),B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha})\big].

(7.12)

We also need to show that, for all
\ell<\ell^{\prime}\leq k
,

\displaystyle B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha})=(\mathfrak{c}_{(\alpha)})^{-1}f_{\alpha}^{\;\gamma\beta}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\beta}),B^{(2\ell+2)}(X_{\gamma})\big].

(7.13)

Again, we proceed by induction on
k
. The base of induction was already shown above. Hence, assuming inductively (
7.13
), we obtain

\displaystyle f_{\alpha}^{\;\gamma\beta}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\beta}),B^{(2\ell+2)}(X_{\gamma})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=(\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\gamma)})^{-1}f_{\alpha}^{\;\gamma\beta}w_{\gamma}^{\;qp}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\beta}),\big[B^{(2\ell^{\prime}+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell-2\ell^{\prime}+1)}(Y_{q})\big]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=(\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\gamma)})^{-1}f_{\alpha}^{\;\gamma\beta}w_{\gamma}^{\;qp}\big(g_{\beta p}^{\;\;\,r}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+2\ell^{\prime}+1)}(Y_{r}),B^{(2\ell-2\ell^{\prime}+1)}(Y_{q})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+g_{\beta q}^{\;\;\,r}\big[B^{(2\ell^{\prime}+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2k-2\ell^{\prime}+1)}(Y_{r})\big]\big)

\displaystyle\qquad{}=(\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\alpha)})^{-1}\mathfrak{c}_{(\alpha)}w_{\alpha}^{\;rp}\big[B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{r})\big].

Here we used (
2.6
), (
2.9
) and (
7.12
). By combining the expression above with (
7.11
) we complete the induction.

We are now in position to prove (
7.6
)–(
7.8
) using induction on
k
. Notice that the base of induction for each of (
7.6
)–(
7.8
) was already shown above. The induction step for (
7.6
) is analogous to the one in Section
7.1
, thus we do not repeat the proof. For (
7.8
), assuming induction, we have

\displaystyle\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\beta}),\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]\big]=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}w^{\;\;\,\alpha}_{pq}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\beta}),B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha})\big].

By (
2.6
), (
2.10
) and (
7.12
) we obtain

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{r}^{\;p\beta}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\beta}),\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{r}^{\;p\beta}g_{\beta p}^{\;\;\,s}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+3)}(Y_{s}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]+\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{r}^{\;p\beta}g_{\beta q}^{\;\;\,\,s}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell+3)}(Y_{s})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+3)}(Y_{r}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]-\tfrac{1}{4}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}\bar{\mathfrak{c}}_{(\beta)}w_{rq}^{\;\;\,\beta}B^{(2k+4)}(X_{\beta}).

By (
2.6
) and (
7.11
) we get

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha,\beta}g_{r}^{\;p\beta}w^{\;\;\,\alpha}_{pq}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\beta}),B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha})\big]=\tfrac{1}{2}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha,\beta,\gamma}w_{rq}^{\;\;\,\gamma}f_{\gamma}^{\;\alpha\beta}\big[B^{(2)}(X_{\beta}),B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha})\big]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha,\beta} g_{r}^{\;p\beta} w^{\;\;\,\alpha}_{pq} \big[B^{(2)}(X_{\beta}),B^{(2k+2)}(X_{\alpha})\big]}{}=\tfrac{1}{4}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\gamma}\mathfrak{c}_{(\gamma)}w_{rq}^{\;\;\,\gamma}B^{(2k+4)}(X_{\gamma})

giving
\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+3)}(Y_{r}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]=\sum_{\beta}w_{rq}^{\;\;\,\beta}B^{(2k+4)}(X_{\beta})
, which combined with (
7.10
) completes the induction.

Finally, for (
7.7
), assuming induction, we have

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{q}^{\;\beta p}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell)}(X_{\beta})\big]\big]=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{q}^{\;\beta p}f_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\,\gamma}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2k)}(X_{\gamma})\big].

(7.14)

Using (
2.10
) and (
7.12
) we compute

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{q}^{\;\beta p}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell)}(X_{\beta})\big]\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{q}^{\;\beta p}g_{p\alpha}^{\;\;\,r}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{r}),B^{(2\ell)}(X_{\beta})\big]+\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{q}^{\;\beta p}g_{p\beta}^{\;\;\,r}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{r})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\gamma}g_{q}^{\;\gamma r}g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,p}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell+1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2\ell)}(X_{\gamma})\big]+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big].

Subtracting the first term in the last equality above from the rhs of (
7.14
) and using (
2.6
), (
7.12
) we get

\displaystyle\textstyle\sum\limits_{\gamma}\Big(\textstyle\sum\limits_{\beta}g_{q}^{\;\beta p}f_{\alpha\beta}^{\;\;\,\gamma}-g_{q}^{\;\gamma r}g_{r\alpha}^{\;\;\,p}\Big)\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2k)}(X_{\gamma})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\textstyle\sum\limits_{\gamma}g_{\alpha q}^{\;\;\,\,r}g_{r}^{\;\gamma p}\big[B^{(1)}(Y_{p}),B^{(2k)}(X_{\gamma})\big]=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}g_{\alpha q}^{\;\;\,\,r}B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{r})

yielding
\big[B^{(2k-2\ell)}(X_{\alpha}),B^{(2\ell+1)}(Y_{q})\big]=g_{\alpha q}^{\;\;\,\,r}B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{r})
, which completes the induction.

Thus we have proved that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{+}=\bigoplus\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(k)}
. By setting
\varphi\colon B^{(2k)}(X_{\alpha})\mapsto X^{(2k)}_{\alpha}
,
B^{(2k+1)}(Y_{p})\mapsto Y^{(2k+1)}_{p}
we obtain a bijection of vector spaces, hence
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{+}\cong{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
.

7.3
A proof of the uniqueness of the co-action of
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g}^{\theta})^{\rm tw}}

We need to show that the map
\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}
given by (
5.15
) together with (
5.16
) is the unique map satisfying properties given by Definition
5.1
and property (4) of Definition
5.3
. The latter property defines the co-action up to the first order in
\hbar
,

\displaystyle\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}(x)=\varphi(x)\otimes 1+1\otimes x+\hbar\tau(x)+{\mathcal{O}}\big(\hbar^{2}\big),

with
x\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h})^{\rm tw}
and
\varphi
the natural embedding
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h})^{\rm tw}\hookrightarrow{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
.

For the degree-
0
generators of
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
the Lie coideal structure is trivial and the minimal form of the co-action is given by

\displaystyle\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}(X_{\alpha})=\varphi(X_{\alpha})\otimes 1+1\otimes X_{\alpha}.

(7.15)

The coideal compatibility identities (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) and the requirement
\varphi(X_{\alpha})|_{\hbar\to 0}=X_{\alpha}
implies that the natural inclusion for
X_{\alpha}
is
\varphi\colon X_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h})^{\rm tw}\mapsto X_{\alpha}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
and that (
7.15
) is indeed the unique co-action satisfying the required properties.

For the degree-
1
generators of
{\mathcal{H}}^{+}
the Lie coideal structure is non-trivial. The minimal co-action is

\displaystyle\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p}))=\varphi(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p}))\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathcal{B}(Y_{p})+\hbar[Y_{p}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{h}}].

(7.16)

As previously, the co-action must satisfy (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) and the requirement
\varphi(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p}))|_{\hbar\to 0}=B(Y_{p})
. By (
5.2
) we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{\hbar}(\varphi(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p})))=\varphi(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p}))\otimes 1+1\otimes\varphi(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p}))+\hbar[Y_{q}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{h}}].

Consider the ansatz
\varphi(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p}))={\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p})+\hbar F^{(0)}_{p}
with some degree-
0
element
F^{(0)}_{p}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
. Recall that
\Delta_{\hbar}({\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p}))={\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p})\otimes 1+1\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p})+\frac{1}{2}\hbar[Y_{p}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]
. We rewrite the equality above as

\displaystyle\Delta_{\hbar}\big({\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p})+\hbar F^{(0)}_{p}\big)-\big(\big({\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p})+\hbar F^{(0)}_{p}\big)\otimes 1+1\otimes\big({\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p})+\hbar F^{(0)}_{p}\big)\big)=\hbar[Y_{p}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{h}}],

which equates to

\displaystyle\Delta_{\hbar}\big(F^{(0)}_{p}\big)-\big(F^{(0)}_{p}\otimes 1+1\otimes F^{(0)}_{p}\big)=\tfrac{1}{2}[Y_{p}\otimes 1,2\Omega_{\mathfrak{h}}-\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\tfrac{1}{2}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}\big(g_{p}^{\;\alpha q}Y_{q}\otimes X_{\alpha}-g_{p}^{\;q\alpha}X_{\alpha}\otimes Y_{q}\big)=\tfrac{1}{2}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\alpha}g_{p}^{\;\alpha q}(Y_{q}\otimes X_{\alpha}+X_{\alpha}\otimes Y_{q}).

Thus we find that
F^{(0)}_{p}=\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\alpha}g_{p}^{\;\alpha q}(Y_{q}X_{\alpha}+X_{\alpha}Y_{q})+cY_{p}=\tfrac{1}{4}[Y_{q},C_{X}]-cY_{p}
for any
c\in\mathbb{C}
, which we can set to
c=0
without loss of generality (this is equivalent to the composition
\varkappa_{-c}\circ\varphi
with the shift-automorphism
\varkappa_{c}\colon{\mathcal{J}}(x)\mapsto{\mathcal{J}}(x)+\hbar cx
of
{\mathcal{Y}}_{\hbar}(\mathfrak{g})
for any
x\in\mathfrak{g}
) giving

\displaystyle\varphi(\mathcal{B}(Y_{p}))={\mathcal{J}}(Y_{p})+\tfrac{1}{4}\hbar[Y_{p},C_{X}],

or in other words is there is a one-parameter family of embeddings that are equivalent to each other via the shift-automorphism of
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
.

It remains to verify that the coideal compatibility identities (
5.1
) and (
5.2
) hold, which imply that (
7.16
) is the unique co-action, and to show that
\varphi
is an algebra homomorphism, namely
\varphi([b_{i},b_{j}])=[\varphi(b_{i}),\varphi(b_{j})]
and
\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}([b_{i},b_{j}])=[\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}(b_{i}),\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}(b_{j})]
for all
b_{i},b_{j}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h})^{\rm tw}
, which follow by a direct computation.

7.4
A proof of the uniqueness of the co-action of
\boldsymbol{{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{\rm tw}}

Similarly as before, we need to show that the map
\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}
given by (
5.23
) and (
5.24
) together with (
5.25
) is the unique map satisfying properties given by Definition
5.1
and property (4) of Definition
5.3
. We first demonstrate an additional lemma that provides us with necessary identities.

Lemma 7.1
.

The following identities hold in
U(\mathfrak{g})
and
U(\mathfrak{g})\otimes U(\mathfrak{g})
:

\displaystyle[[x_{i}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]=\tfrac{1}{2}(\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac})(x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}-\{x_{b},x_{c}\}\otimes x_{a}),

(7.17)

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}[[x_{k}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],[x_{j}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]]=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}(x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}+\{x_{b},x_{c}\}\otimes x_{a}),

(7.18)

\displaystyle 6\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\pi}\alpha_{j}^{\;\pi(c)r}\alpha_{k}^{\;\pi(b)s}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,\pi(a)}+\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\big(\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac}\big).

(7.19)

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle[a\otimes b,c\otimes d]=[a,c]\otimes\{b,d\}+\{a,c\}\otimes[b,d].

For the first identity we have

\displaystyle[[x_{i}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]=\alpha_{i}^{\;bj}\eta^{kc}[x_{j}\otimes x_{b},x_{k}\otimes x_{c}]

\displaystyle\hphantom{[[x_i\otimes 1,\Omega_\mathfrak{g}],\Omega_\mathfrak{g}]}{}=\alpha_{i}^{\;bj}\eta^{kc}(\alpha_{jk}^{\;\;\,a}x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}+\alpha_{bc}^{\;\;\,a}\{x_{k},x_{j}\}\otimes x_{a})

\displaystyle\hphantom{[[x_i\otimes 1,\Omega_\mathfrak{g}],\Omega_\mathfrak{g}]}{}=\alpha_{i}^{\;bj}\eta^{kc}(\alpha_{jk}^{\;\;\,a}x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}-\alpha_{jk}^{\;\;\,a}\{x_{c},x_{b}\}\otimes x_{a})\qquad\text{by ren. }k,j\leftrightarrow c,b

\displaystyle\hphantom{[[x_i\otimes 1,\Omega_\mathfrak{g}],\Omega_\mathfrak{g}]}{}=\alpha_{i}^{\;bj}\alpha_{j}^{\;ca}(x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}-\{x_{c},x_{b}\}\otimes x_{a}).

For the second identity we have

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}[[x_{k}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],[x_{j}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]]=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{k}^{\;br}\alpha_{j}^{\;cs}[x_{r}\otimes x_{b},x_{s}\otimes x_{c}]

\displaystyle\hphantom{\alpha_i^{\;jk} [[x_k\otimes 1,\Omega_\mathfrak{g}],[x_j\otimes 1,\Omega_\mathfrak{g}]]}{}=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cs}\alpha_{k}^{\;br}(\alpha_{rs}^{\;\;\,a}x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}+\alpha_{bc}^{\;\;\,a}\{x_{s},x_{r}\}\otimes x_{a})

\displaystyle\hphantom{\alpha_i^{\;jk} [[x_k\otimes 1,\Omega_\mathfrak{g}],[x_j\otimes 1,\Omega_\mathfrak{g}]]}{}=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cs}\alpha_{k}^{\;br}\alpha_{rs}^{\;\;\,a}(x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}+\{x_{c},x_{b}\}\otimes x_{a})

by renaming
b,c\leftrightarrow r,s
. The third identity is obtained using the following auxiliary identities:

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;as}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,b}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab},

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{j}^{\;ar}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,c}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac}.

The first auxiliary identity follows by multiple application of the Jacobi identity. In particular,

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\big(\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}-\alpha_{k}^{\;as}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,b}\big)=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{rk}^{\;\;\,s}\alpha_{s}^{\;ab}=\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jr}\alpha_{j}^{\;kc}\big)\alpha_{rk}^{\;\;\,s}\alpha_{s}^{\;ab}\\
&\hphantom{\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}(\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}-\alpha_{k}^{\;as}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,b})}{}=\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;kr}\alpha_{rk}^{\;\;\,s}\alpha_{s}^{\;ab}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}.\end{split}

The second auxiliary identity follows by the
b\leftrightarrow c
symmetry and renaming
j,s\leftrightarrow k,r
in the first term on the right hand side. We also have that

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\pi}\alpha_{j}^{\;\pi(c)r}\alpha_{k}^{\;\pi(b)s}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,\pi(a)}=2\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\big(\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}+\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;as}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,b}+\alpha_{j}^{\;ar}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,c}\big).

Hence taking a double sum of the auxiliary identities and adding
2\alpha_{j}^{\;cr}\alpha_{k}^{\;bs}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,a}
to both sides of the resulting equality reproduces (
7.19
) as required.
∎

The degree-0 generators have a trivial Lie coideal structure, hence, by the same arguments as before,

\displaystyle\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}(x_{i})=\varphi(x_{i})\otimes 1+1\otimes x_{i},

and
\varphi\colon x_{i}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{\rm tw}\mapsto x_{i}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
is the natural inclusion. For the degree-2 generators we have

\displaystyle\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))=\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))\otimes 1+1\otimes\mathcal{G}(x_{i})+\hbar[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i})\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]+\hbar^{2}W^{(0)}_{i},

with
W^{(0)}_{i}
being a degree-0 element in
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{\rm tw}
, which can also be viewed as an element in
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})\otimes{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
via the natural inclusion. Coinvariance (
5.2
) implies

\displaystyle\Delta_{\hbar}\big(\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))\big)=\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))\otimes 1+1\otimes\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))+\hbar[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i})\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]+\hbar^{2}W^{(0)}_{i}.

(7.20)

We also must have
\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))|_{\hbar\to 0}=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}[J(x_{k}),J(x_{j})]
. Let
K^{(2)}_{i}=\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{k}),{\mathcal{J}}(x_{j})]
and choose the ansatz
\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))=K^{(2)}_{i}+\hbar F^{(1)}_{i}
with some degree-1 element
F^{(1)}_{i}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
. (We do not need to consider degree-0 elements in the ansatz since all
x_{i}\in{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{g})^{\rm tw}
.) We then have

\displaystyle\Delta_{\hbar}\big(K^{(2)}_{i}\big)=K^{(2)}_{i}\otimes 1+1\otimes K^{(2)}_{i}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\Delta_\hbar\big(K^{(2)}_i\big)=}{}+\tfrac{1}{2}\hbar[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i})\otimes 1-1\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i}),\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]+\tfrac{1}{4}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\hbar^{2}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}[[x_{k}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],[x_{j}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]],

(7.21)

which is obtained by making use of the following computation

\displaystyle\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{k})\otimes 1+1\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{k}),[x_{j},\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]]=\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}\alpha_{kb}^{\;\;\,c}({\mathcal{J}}(x_{c})\otimes x_{a}-x_{a}\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{c}))

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}(\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}\alpha_{kb}^{\;\;\,c}-\alpha_{k}^{\;ab}\alpha_{jb}^{\;\;\,c})({\mathcal{J}}(x_{c})\otimes x_{a}-x_{a}\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{c}))

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\alpha_{kj}^{\;\;\,b}\alpha_{b}^{\;ac}({\mathcal{J}}(x_{c})\otimes x_{a}-x_{a}\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{c}))

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}\alpha_{i}^{\;ac}({\mathcal{J}}(x_{c})\otimes x_{a}-x_{a}\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{c}))=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i})\otimes 1-1\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i}),\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}].

By comparing the terms linear in
\hbar
in (
7.20
) and (
7.21
) we find
F^{(1)}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{4}[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i}),C_{\mathfrak{g}}]
. Note that

\displaystyle\Delta_{\hbar}\big(F^{(1)}_{i}\big)=F^{(1)}_{i}\otimes 1+1\otimes F^{(1)}_{i}+\tfrac{1}{2}[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i})\otimes 1+1\otimes{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i}),\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]+\tfrac{1}{4}\hbar[[x_{i}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}].

Lastly, by comparing the terms quadratic in
\hbar
in (
7.20
) and using the expressions for
K^{(2)}_{i}
and
F^{(1)}_{i}
we find

\displaystyle 4W^{(0)}_{i}=[[x_{i}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]+\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}[[x_{k}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}],[x_{j}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]].

as required.

The coideal co-associativity identity (
5.1
) in this case is not that straightforward, thus we demonstrate it explicitly. We need to show that the expression below equates to zero:

\displaystyle\big((\Delta_{\hbar}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar})\circ\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}\big)(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))

\displaystyle\qquad{}=\big(\Delta_{\hbar}(\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i})))-\big(\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))\otimes 1+1\otimes\varphi(\mathcal{G}(x_{i}))+\hbar[{\mathcal{J}}(x_{i})\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}]\big)\big)\otimes 1

\displaystyle\qquad\quad{}+\hbar^{2}\big((\Delta_{\hbar}\otimes\operatorname{id})\big(W^{(0)}_{i}\big)-(\operatorname{id}\otimes\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar})\big(W^{(0)}_{i}\big)-1\otimes W^{(0)}_{i}+\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}([x_{k}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}])\otimes x_{j}\big).

Using the fact
\varphi(x_{i})=x_{i}
the relation above gives a constraint

\displaystyle(\Delta_{\hbar}\otimes\operatorname{id})\big(W^{(0)}_{i}\big)-(\operatorname{id}\otimes\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar})\big(W^{(0)}_{i}\big)+W^{(0)}_{i}\otimes 1-1\otimes W^{(0)}_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad{}+\tfrac{1}{2}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}([x_{k}\otimes 1,\Omega_{\mathfrak{g}}])\otimes x_{j}=0.

(7.22)

By Lemma (
7.1
) the explicit form of
W^{(0)}_{i}
is equal to

\displaystyle W^{(0)}_{i}=\tfrac{1}{8}\big(\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac}\big)(x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}-\{x_{b},x_{c}\}\otimes x_{a})

\displaystyle\hphantom{W^{(0)}_i =}{}+\tfrac{1}{24}\Big(\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\pi}\alpha_{j}^{\;\pi(c)r}\alpha_{k}^{\;\pi(b)s}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,\pi(a)}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac}\Big)

\displaystyle\hphantom{W^{(0)}_i =}{}\times(x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}+\{x_{b},x_{c}\}\otimes x_{a})

\displaystyle\hphantom{W^{(0)}_i}{}=\tfrac{1}{12}\big(\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac}\big)(2x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}-\{x_{b},x_{c}\}\otimes x_{a})

\displaystyle\hphantom{W^{(0)}_i=}{}+\tfrac{1}{24}\mathfrak{c}_{\mathfrak{g}}^{-1}\alpha_{i}^{\;jk}\textstyle\sum\limits_{\pi}\alpha_{j}^{\;\pi(c)r}\alpha_{k}^{\;\pi(b)s}\alpha_{sr}^{\;\;\,\pi(a)}(x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}+\{x_{b},x_{c}\}\otimes x_{a}).

(7.23)

Denote
W^{(0)}_{i}=h_{i}^{\;abc}x_{a}\otimes\{x_{b},x_{c}\}+\overline{h}_{i}^{\;abc}\{x_{b},x_{c}\}\otimes x_{a}
. Then (
7.22
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle 4\overline{h}_{i}^{\;abc}x_{c}\otimes x_{b}\otimes x_{a}-4h_{i}^{\;abc}x_{a}\otimes x_{b}\otimes x_{c}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}x_{a}\otimes x_{b}\otimes x_{c}=0,

giving
4\overline{h}_{i}^{\;cba}-4h_{i}^{\;abc}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}=0
. Using (
7.23
) we find

\displaystyle 4\overline{h}_{i}^{\;cba}-4h_{i}^{\;abc}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}=-\tfrac{1}{3}\big(\alpha_{i}^{\;ja}\alpha_{j}^{\;cb}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ca}\big)-\tfrac{2}{3}\big(\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac}\big)+\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}

\displaystyle\hphantom{4\lx@text@overline{h}_i^{\;cba} - 4 h_i^{\;abc} + \alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_j^{\;ab}}{}=-\tfrac{1}{3}\big(\alpha_{i}^{\;ja}\alpha_{j}^{\;cb}+\alpha_{i}^{\;jb}\alpha_{j}^{\;ac}\big)+\tfrac{1}{3}\alpha_{i}^{\;jc}\alpha_{j}^{\;ab}=0

by the co-Jacobi identity. By reversing the computations above and using (
5.2
) one can deduce that co-action
\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}
is the unique map satisfying the required properties up to the shift
(\varkappa_{c}\otimes\operatorname{id})\circ\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{}}{}
{}{}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -6.4 L 7.87 2.95}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}
, i.e., there exists a one-parameter family of co-actions that are equivalent to each other via the shift-automorphism
\varkappa_{c}
of
{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathfrak{g})
.

It remains to verify that the map
\varphi
is an algebra homomorphism,
\varphi([b_{i},b_{j}])=[\varphi(b_{i}),\varphi(b_{j})]
and
\!\!\mbox{
\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
{}{}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}\hskip 0.7113pt_{\hbar}([b_{i},b_{j}])=[\!\!\mbox{

\hbox to6.49pt{\vbox to7.56pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.4pt\lower-5.02351pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}{{}}{}
